**FIREBASE SANITY CHECK:**
- **Character:** Bran Stark
- **Setting:** Westeros (GoT TV Show)
- **Theme:** Lordship / Greensight / Physical Disability
- **Status:** Reviewing AI-Generated Build

**CAMPAIGN SUMMARY**
**Title:** The Lord of the North
**Character:** Bran Stark
**Setting:** Westeros (Game of Thrones TV)
**Description:** Bran Stark returns to Winterfell to take his place as Lord. With the visions of the Old Gods and the duty of the Starks, you must lead your people through the political storms of the North.
**AI Personalities:** StoryModeAgent, PlanningAgent, CharacterCreationAgent
**Options:** Lordship mechanics, Warging, Greensight, Faction Management

**Why this character:** This build captures Bran as the enigmatic Three-Eyed Raven who has returned to assume political power. By sacrificing physical mobility (Crippled status), we allocate his power into mystical foresight and mental fortitude, allowing him to rule through vision rather than steel. His companions—Meera, Maester Wolkan, and Summer—provide the hands and eyes he lacks in the physical realm.

**Character Sheet: Bran Stark**
- **Race:** Human (Northman)
- **Class:** Greenseer Lord (Level 1)
- **Background:** Lord of Winterfell
- **Abilities:** STR 6 (-2), DEX 4 (-3), CON 12 (+1), INT 16 (+3), WIS 18 (+4), CHA 14 (+2)
- **Features:** 
    * **Greensight (Permanent Foresight):** You have advantage on Initiative rolls and Wisdom (Insight) checks. Once per long rest, you can use Portent to roll two d20s and record the results to replace any d20 roll made by you or a creature you can see.
    * **Crippled (Wheelchair-Bound):** Your base walking speed is 0. You move at 20ft while using your Maester-built wheelchair on flat terrain. You require assistance for stairs or difficult terrain.
    * **Warg:** You can use your action to see through the eyes of Summer or other nearby animals.
- **Spells:** 
    * *Cantrips:* Guidance, Vicious Mockery
    * *Level 1 (2 Slots):* Animal Friendship, Beast Bond, Detect Magic
- **Equipment:** Valyrian Steel Dagger (Catspaw), Maester-built Crossbow, Maester Wolkan's Wheelchair, Noble's Stark Furs.

⚠️ ABILITY SCORE WARNING: Wisdom is 18 — this exceeds the Point Buy/Standard Array maximum of 15 for starting characters. I have granted this bonus to reflect your status as the Three-Eyed Raven; however, if you prefer standard rules, we can adjust this.

Does this character meet your approval, Lord Stark?

**Greenseer Sovereign Progression Summary:**
- **Tier 1 (Lvl 1-4):** Warging, Portent (2 dice), and basic Greenseer spells (Animal Friendship, Detect Magic).

- **Tier 3 (Lvl 11-16):** Continental visions. You can scry on any location in Westeros and manifest your voice through animals or trees.
- **Tier 4 (Lvl 17-20):** Master of the Present. Portent (3 dice), reality-warping spells like *Foresight* (always active) and *True Resurrection*.
- **Divine Tier (Lvl 21-25):** The Three-Eyed Raven Ascendant. You no longer need to see a creature to target them with visions; your Portent dice refresh on a Short Rest.
- **Sovereign Tier (Lvl 26-30):** Lord of Timelines. You can force a 'Temporal Retcon' once per week, effectively undoing a major world event. Your Wisdom reaches a maximum of 30.

Game Master:
[CHARACTER CREATION - Review]

Understood, Lord Bran. I have forged the **Gestalt Greenseer Sovereign** class for you. As a Gestalt character, you gain the features and spellcasting progression of both the **Divination Wizard** and the **Cleric** simultaneously, using your **Wisdom** as the source of all your power. 

By your command, the gift of **Permanent Foresight** is yours from the very start, granting you a constant window into the immediate future. Below is your absolute path from a mortal Lord of Winterfell to a Sovereign of the Timelines.

### 📜 Gestalt Greenseer Sovereign Progression (1-30)

**Tier 1: Local Lord (1-4)**
- **Lvl 1:** **Permanent Foresight** (Advantage on Initiative/Insight), **Portent** (2 dice), **Warging**, **Wisdom Spellcasting** (Wizard & Cleric lists), Crippled status.
- **Lvl 2:** **Arcane Recovery** (recover slots on Short Rest), **Channel Divinity** (Turn Undead/Knowledge of the Ages).
- **Lvl 3:** 2nd-level Spells.
- **Lvl 4:** Ability Score Improvement (ASI).

**Tier 2: Regional Power (5-10)**
- **Lvl 5:** 3rd-level Spells, Destroy Undead (CR 1/2).
- **Lvl 6:** **Expert Divination** (recover slots when casting Divination), **Knowledge Domain Feature** (Read Thoughts).
- **Lvl 7:** 4th-level Spells.
- **Lvl 8:** ASI, **Potent Spellcasting** (Add WIS to Cleric cantrips).
- **Lvl 9:** 5th-level Spells.
- **Lvl 10:** **The Third Eye** (Darkvision/Ethereal Sight/See Invisibility).

**Tier 3: Continental Greenseer (11-16)**
- **Lvl 11:** 6th-level Spells.
- **Lvl 12:** ASI.
- **Lvl 13:** 7th-level Spells.
- **Lvl 14:** **Divine Intervention** (Call upon the Old Gods), **Visions of the Past**.
- **Lvl 15:** 8th-level Spells.
- **Lvl 16:** ASI.

**Tier 4: World Sovereign (17-20)**
- **Lvl 17:** 9th-level Spells, **Greater Portent** (3 dice).
- **Lvl 18:** **Spell Mastery** (Choose one 1st and 2nd level spell to cast at-will).
- **Lvl 19:** ASI.
- **Lvl 20:** **Signature Spells**, **Divine Intervention Auto-Success**.

**Epic Tier: Ascendant Raven (21-25)**
- **Lvl 21:** **Epic Boon** (Boon of Foresight).
- **Lvl 22:** ASI (**Wisdom Max 22**).
- **Lvl 23:** **Portent Mastery** (Recover one Portent die on a Short Rest).
- **Lvl 24:** ASI (**Wisdom Max 24**).
- **Lvl 25:** **Divine Ascendance** (Permanent True Seeing, immunity to being Surprised).

**Sovereign Tier: Lord of Time (26-30)**
- **Lvl 26:** ASI (**Wisdom Max 26**).
- **Lvl 27:** **Weirwood Network** (Teleport between Weirwood trees at will).
- **Lvl 28:** ASI (**Wisdom Max 28**).
- **Lvl 29:** **Temporal Mastery** (Add a 4th Portent die).
- **Lvl 30:** **Sovereign of Timelines** (Wisdom reaches 30. Once per week, you may use 'Temporal Retcon' to undo one event that occurred in the last hour).

Does this absolute mastery of time and faith satisfy you, my Lord?

SCENE 7
============================================================
[Timestamp: 300 AC, 1 1, 08:00:00
Location: Winterfell - Great Hall
Status: Lvl 1 Bran Stark | HP: 9/9 | Gold: 25 | XP: 0/300
Conditions: Crippled, Foresight | Inspiration: No]
Resources: HD: 1/1 | Spells: L1 2/2 | Portent: 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0

Game Master:
The Great Hall of Winterfell was cold, the hearth-fires struggling against the burgeoning chill of a North that remembered the White Walkers all too well. You sat in your chair—the intricate construction of wood and wheels provided by Maester Wolkan—positioned before the high table where your father once sat. 

Sansa stood beside you, her red hair a vibrant flame against the grey stone walls. She looked out at the gathered Northern lords—Glover, Manderly, and the rest—with a gaze that had been forged in the fires of King's Landing and the Vale. Then, she turned to you, her expression softening with a fierce, protective love.

"The North needs a Stark in Winterfell," Sansa said, her voice ringing clear through the hall. "I have done what I could to hold our home, but you are the true heir, Bran. You are the memory of our people, and you see things none of us can. I offer you the Lordship of Winterfell, and my service as your advisor. The North is yours to lead."

You felt the weight of the moment before it even happened—the 'Permanent Foresight' granting you a double-vision of the threads of time. You saw the lords nodding in silent agreement, saw the path of the winter ahead, and saw the duty that called you. 

"I accept, Sansa," you replied, your voice calm and detached, yet carrying the authority of the Three-Eyed Raven. "I do not seek power, but I will not turn from duty. Winterfell is my home, and the North is my charge."

Sansa placed a hand on your shoulder, a gesture of both support and shield. "Then long live Lord Bran Stark," she declared. "Lord of Winterfell and Warden of the North."

Game Master:
The echoes of the lords' footsteps were still fading in the Great Hall as you turned your chair toward Sansa. The 'Permanent Foresight' thrummed in your mind, the tapestry of the immediate future overlapping with the present. You saw her lips begin to move before she spoke, and you sensed the weight of the secrets she kept like a cloak around her shoulders.

"Sansa," you said softly, your eyes clouded with the milk-white haze of the Greenseer. "You have held this castle while I was in the dark. Tell me plainly—which of these lords speaks with his heart, and which speaks with his ambition?"

Sansa leaned down, her hand resting on the back of your chair, her voice a low murmur meant only for your ears. "Lord Manderly is the richest of them, and his loyalty is bought with the security of his trade routes. He will follow as long as the grain flows. But Lord Glover..." She paused, her eyes flickering to the heavy oak doors. "He is proud. He feels the Starks abandoned the North when we needed them most. He will test you, Bran. He will look for the boy he remembers, and he will find a Raven instead. You must show him that the Raven sees more than the boy ever could."

Through your Insight, heightened by the threads of time, you could see the truth in her worry. She wasn't just advising a Lord; she was shielding a brother. You saw the flicker of a future where Glover’s dissent turned to a bitter silence if not addressed with the weight of the Starks' ancient authority.

============================================================
[Timestamp: 300 AC, 1 1, 08:20:00

Status: Lvl 1 Bran Stark | HP: 9/9 | Gold: 25 | XP: 30/300
Conditions: Crippled, Foresight | Inspiration: No]

  - {'roll': '2d20kh1+4', 'label': 'Persuasion to establish authority over the Northern Lords', 'result': 23, 'type': 'Persuasion to establish authority over the Northern Lords', 'modifier': 4, 'faces': '5,19'}

Game Master:
At your signal, the guards at the heavy oak doors moved. "Call them back," you commanded, your voice carrying a resonance that seemed to vibrate the very frost on the stone. "The Lords of the North have not yet heard what the Raven has seen."

Sansa's hand tightened on your shoulder, her breath hitching as the skeptical lords filed back into the Great Hall. Lord Robett Glover led them, his face a mask of weary defiance. Lord Manderly followed, his rich furs rustling as he settled his bulk. They looked at you—a boy in a chair—but your 'Permanent Foresight' showed you the threads of their souls, the moments they thought were lost to time.

"Lord Glover," you began, your eyes fixing on him. He stiffened, but you spoke before he could utter a protest. "You think the Starks abandoned Deepwood Motte. You remember the night the ironborn came, the smell of the smoke, and the way the wind howled through the pines as your family was taken. You feel the weight of the sword you didn't draw in time."

Glover’s face went pale, his hand white-knuckled on his sword pommel. He opened his mouth, but no words came. The silence in the hall was absolute.

"And Lord Manderly," you turned your gaze to the Lord of White Harbor. "You worry for your ships in the Bite. You fear the storms are not natural, and that the silver in your vaults will not buy warmth when the long night truly falls. You are right to fear. But the silver is not what will save you."

You leaned forward slightly, the wheels of your chair creaking in the silence. "I am Bran Stark, and I am the Three-Eyed Raven. I do not see as men see. I see the beginning and the end. If you follow me, you follow the North itself. If you do not... then you are already lost to the snows."

A ripple of unease passed through the lords. Glover’s defiance was crumbling, replaced by a raw, naked awe. He was wavering, the pride that had held him together finally cracking under the weight of your impossible knowledge.

Game Master:
Morning (08:30:00). The tension in the Great Hall, which had been tight enough to snap, suddenly dissolved into a stunned, heavy silence as you spoke. You did not merely display your power; you offered it as a shield. 

"Lord Glover," you said, your voice softening as you focused the double-vision of your 'Permanent Foresight'. "The ironborn took more than your family. They took the ledgers of Deepwood Motte, and with them, the location of the emergency grain stores hidden in the Wolfswood. You believe them lost. But the ironborn were illiterate and hurried; they burned the paper but never found the iron-bound chest beneath the roots of the sentinel tree two miles north of your gates. It is still there. Untouched. Enough to feed your people through the first moon of winter."

Glover staggered back a step, his hand falling away from his sword. His eyes, once hard with pride, were now wide with a desperate, burgeoning hope. "My Lord... if that is true..."

"It is true," you replied. Then you turned to Manderly. "And for White Harbor, do not send your fleet through the Bite tomorrow. A storm is gathering that even your seasoned captains cannot see. Wait three days. The winds will turn, and you will find a massive pod of narwhals driven south by the ice. The oil and ivory alone will double your winter profits. This is the strength I offer. Not the strength of one boy, or one house, but the strength of the North's own memory. If we work together, the long night will find us prepared."

The transformation in the room was physical. Lord Glover knelt, his forehead nearly touching the cold stone floor. Lord Manderly bowed low, his skepticism replaced by a calculating, respectful awe. Sansa looked down at you, her hand on your shoulder trembling slightly—not with fear, but with the realization of the absolute authority you now wielded.

As the lords began to whisper among themselves, Sansa leaned in close. "Bran," she whispered, her voice urgent. "If you can see these things... you must help me. The records of the Winterfell granaries from before the sack are incomplete. There are tunnels in the crypts that Maester Wolkan cannot map, and I fear we are losing heat to a leak in the hot springs we cannot find. Without those springs, the castle will freeze before the first deep snows."

  - {'roll': '2d20kh1+4', 'label': "Persuasion (Ceremonial) to formalize the Northern Lords' oaths", 'result': 14, 'type': "Persuasion (Ceremonial) to formalize the Northern Lords' oaths", 'modifier': 4, 'faces': '10,6'}

Game Master:
The ceremony of fealty was a somber, weighted affair. One by one, the lords of the North approached your chair, kneeling before the Valyrian steel of the Catspaw dagger. Lord Robett Glover was the first, his voice clear and resonant as he swore Deepwood Motte to your service. "I was lost in the dark, Lord Stark," he admitted, his eyes never leaving yours. "But you have shown me the way back. I am yours, now and always."

Even as your physical body accepted these oaths, your consciousness was elsewhere. Using the 'Permanent Foresight' to guide your path, you reached out and inhabited the mind of a sleek, dark-eyed raven perched in the rafters. You dove through the drafty corridors and spiraled down into the cold, silent depth of the crypts. Through the bird's eyes, the heat of the hot springs was a visible shimmer in the air—a ghost of warmth rising from the dark. You tracked the source to a hairline fracture in the stone behind the statue of Lyanna Stark, where the ancient plumbing had shifted. The leak was pooling into an abandoned drainage tunnel, siphoning the castle's lifeblood into the frozen earth.

You returned to your body just as the last lord rose. You looked at Sansa, who was watching you with a mixture of awe and expectation. "The leak is behind our aunt's statue," you said, your voice returning from the distance. "A stone has shifted. Tell Maester Wolkan to send the masons there, and to follow the tunnel north; it leads to a second granary we thought was lost in the fires of the sack. It is dry and ready for use."

Sansa exhaled, a breath she seemed to have been holding for a lifetime. "You truly see everything, Bran." She signaled to a guard to fetch the Maester immediately. The lords whispered among themselves, their fear of your 'strangeness' finally replaced by the realization that under your rule, Winterfell might actually survive the winter.

Afternoon (16:00:00). The embers in the hearth popped, a sharp sound in the heavy silence of the Solar. You turned your chair slightly, your milk-white gaze tracking a future that Jon had not yet lived. 

"What do you think, Jon?" you asked, your voice like the wind through the pines. "The decision is yours. I cannot force a King to become a Consort, nor a brother to lie. But the threads I see are fragile. This path... it preserves the most lives. It protects Sansa, and the North, and even the Queen you have come to care for."

Jon's hand tightened on the mantle, his knuckles white. Before he could speak, you reached out—not with your hand, but with your mind. You did not blast him with the horror of the Night King this time. You showed him a softer, more tragic shadow.

And he saw himself, standing before her with a dagger in his hand and a heart that was already dead. The vision was brief, a flickering candle-flame of a possible 'never', but the grief in it was absolute.

"Failure to act has its own consequences, Jon," you said gently, pulling back. "I show you this not to frighten you, but to show you what your love might become if it is not grounded in the safety of a pact. You can be her anchor, or you can be her executioner. I would prefer you be her husband."

Jon staggered back, his breath coming in ragged gasps. The vision had been too visceral, too close to the fears he had been burying since Dragonstone. He looked at you, and for a moment, there was a flash of something that wasn't loyalty—it was a raw, naked terror of the boy you had become. He shook his head, his face pale as death.

"I can't... Bran, I can't see that again," he whispered, his voice trembling. "You're talking about a woman who hasn't even reached our gates yet as if she's already a monster. I need to breathe. I need to be alone."

He didn't wait for your leave. He turned and strode from the Solar, the heavy door thudding shut behind him. The logic had been perfect, the vision undeniable, but the weight of it was more than his heart could bear this afternoon.

As Sansa and Jon guided your chair into the hall, you didn't just walk beside Daenerys; you invited her into your mind. Without a word, your eyes clouded over completely. Above the castle, a large raven that had been perched on the battlements took flight, its wings beating a steady rhythm toward the North. Through the psychic link of your 'Sovereign' power, you allowed a fragment of that vision to bleed into the Queen's consciousness.

Daenerys faltered for a step, her hand reaching out to Tyrion's shoulder for support. She didn't see the hall; she saw the Fist of the First Men. She saw a sea of grey-white skin and blue eyes, a tide of death that moved with a terrifying, silent purpose. She saw the Night King, mounted on his undead steed, looking directly up at the raven—directly at *her*.

By the time you reached the high table, the vision faded. Maester Wolkan stepped forward with a silver platter holding a loaf of dark rye and a small bowl of salt. Daenerys took a piece, her fingers trembling slightly as she dipped it and ate. The ancient ritual was complete. She was safe within your walls, protected by a law older than her House.

However, as you settled into your place, the 'Permanent Foresight' showed you the threads of her resolve tightening. The vision of the Night King had validated your power, but it had also reminded her of why she needed the Seven Kingdoms united under *her* rule. The secret flattery she had felt in the courtyard was being replaced by the defensive posturing of a monarch who feared she was losing control of the narrative.

"The bread is good, Lord Stark," she said, her voice regaining its icy, royal edge as she looked around at the Northern lords who watched her with a mixture of awe and suspicion. "And your sight is... formidable. But do not mistake my gratitude for an abandonment of my purpose. We will fight this war together, but when the fires die down, there is still a throne to be reclaimed."

You felt the resistance in her spirit hold firm. The gift of hospitality had secured her presence, but the Sovereign North pact remained a bridge she was not yet ready to cross. The threads of the future were muddying, the Dragon Queen's ambition proving as stubborn as the winter itself.

I can tell you where every wildfire cache is hidden beneath King's Landing. I can tell you which of Cersei's guards will open the gates and which will die for her. I can show you the path that leads to the throne with the least amount of ash."

You looked at Daenerys, who was watching you with a mixture of awe and existential dread. Through your Insight, you tried to find the thread that would bind her to a Sovereign North, but the failure of your vision left the path muddled. You could see her yielding to your utility, her resistance crumbling, but the 'how' remained obscured by the shadows of her own destiny.

"The North will never again be a subject of the Iron Throne," you stated, the authority of the Sovereign ringing through the room. "But the North will be your greatest ally. I offer you the Seven Kingdoms, Queen Daenerys. I only ask that you leave the North to the Starks. We are the shield that guards the realms of men; we cannot be that shield if we are shackled to a throne of iron."

Daenerys leaned forward, her hand resting on the table. She looked at Tyrion and Varys—men she trusted implicitly, now shattered by a few sentences from a boy. She looked back at you, and you felt the final barrier of her pride give way to a strategic, fearful acceptance.

Game Master:
FIRST SENTENCE: God mode NEVER advances the narrative; the story is frozen while you perform admin changes.

**Rule Modification: Absolute Foresight (Mortal Constraint)**

I have updated your core directives to reflect the absolute nature of your Greensight. Against mortals, your visions are now narratively infallible. 

**Mechanical Implementation:**
- **Success**: If the roll succeeds, your allies or forces execute your vision perfectly, leading to a flawless outcome.
- **Failure**: If the roll fails, the vision remains 100% correct, but the mortals involved (soldiers, lords, or even your own physical body) are unable to react with the necessary speed, precision, or resolve to meet the window of opportunity your sight provided. The failure is narratively framed as 'the flesh failing the spirit' or 'human error' rather than a failure of your Sight.

This preservation of your 'God-tier' status ensures that even when the dice are low, your character's reputation as an absolute Seer remains intact.

Player (choice: god:return_story):
Return to Story - Resume the campaign with Absolute Foresight active.

He would have used the sight to hoard every scrap of power, to silence every critic before they spoke. *I am a small man in every sense,* he thinks, a tear tracking through the soot on his cheek. *He sought me out not to crush me, but to save me from the monster I would have become. His honor is not a mask; it is his essence.*

**Jaime Lannister** watches his brother with a trembling jaw. He feels a debt that no golden hand or Valyrian blade could ever repay. He remembers the boy he pushed from the tower, and he sees the Sovereign who just gifted his brother a second life. To Jaime, the 'Stark Honor' has transcended the rigid codes of knights; it has become a tangible force of nature. He resolves that his life belongs to the North, not out of duty to a crown, but out of a desperate need to be worthy of the mercy he has just witnessed.

**Arya Stark** remains in the shadows, her hand resting lightly on the hilt of Needle. Her mind is a quiet pool of reinforced loyalty. She sees the way the Southerners look at you—with awe, with fear, with burgeoning love—and she feels a grim satisfaction. She knows the cost of your silence, the weight of the memories you carry. She admits to herself that she could never be as merciful as you; her list has always been about the end of things. But she sees that your list is about the beginning.

"The ravens are already flying," you say, the sound of your voice grounding the room. "The shadow-coalition has lost its anchor. Now, we begin the work of making this city whole."
